I’m not really sure, honestly. I don’t usually, like, time myself or anything. Sometimes it feels like I look down and a bunch of it is just gone, but I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not paying attention.

Like at work, I’m always having to eat on breaks kind of quick anyway, because if the line gets backed up they’ll call somebody over, and then I’m thinking about that kind of stuff even when I’m home. But during those takeout times… I guess I haven’t really noticed the speed so much. I notice more that I’m just kind of doing it.

Yeah… that’s kind of why I’m here. After my shifts at the register, I’ll stop and buy these huge takeout orders—like, not just one meal, it’s burgers and fries and chicken, sometimes desserts too, like it’s for a whole family.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much it. Once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll be in my head saying, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hands are already reaching for the next thing anyway.

It’s weird because I can know I should stop, like really know it, and still keep going. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.

Sometimes I’m thinking about the receipt, or if the fries got cold, or some random customer from work who was rude about coupons… but I’m still eating. So yeah, stopping doesn’t really feel like something I’m in charge of once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I’ll be sitting there telling myself to stop, like, “Demetrius, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already grabbing the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s like after the first bite something just shuts off in my head. And then I’m thinking about other random stuff too, like whether I still have my name tag in my pocket or if the fries are getting cold, but I’m still eating. So… yeah, I don’t feel like I’m really controlling how much once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I don’t usually, like, time myself or anything. Sometimes it feels like I look down and a bunch of it is just gone, but I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not paying attention.\n\nLike at work, I’m always having to eat on breaks kind of quick anyway, because if the line gets backed up they’ll call somebody over, and then I’m thinking about that kind of stuff even when I’m home. But during those takeout times… I guess I haven’t really noticed the speed so much. I notice more that I’m just kind of doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of why I’m here. After my shifts at the register, I’ll stop and buy these huge takeout orders—like, not just one meal, it’s burgers and fries and chicken, sometimes desserts too, like it’s for a whole family.\n\nAnd I’ll tell myself I’m just gonna have some of it before I head home, but then I’m sitting there in the car or wherever, and most of it’s gone. It’s embarrassing, because I’ll be thinking about how the person at the counter probably knows it’s all for me, or how I should’ve just gone straight home after work… but yeah, it’s a lot of food, and it happens pretty quick.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself from continuing to eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much it. Once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll be in my head saying, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hands are already reaching for the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can know I should stop, like really know it, and still keep going. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess it’s the eating. And then I’ll sit there and eat almost all of it myself before I even go home.\n\nIt’s weird because at work I’m scanning groceries all day, and I see people buying normal dinners, you know, and I’ll be thinking about what I’m gonna get after. Anyway… that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating these large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Like once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already reaching for the next thing.\n\nAnd it’s not like I’m even enjoying every bite, you know? Sometimes I’m thinking about the receipt, or if the fries got cold, or some random customer from work who was rude about coupons… but I’m still eating. So yeah, stopping doesn’t really feel like something I’m in charge of once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: No… not really. And then I’m thinking about other random stuff too, like whether I still have my name tag in my pocket or if the fries are getting cold, but I’m still eating. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. Like, for the last several months, probably three or four nights in most weeks.\n\nI keep trying to tell myself it was just a bad week, but then another week goes by and it happens again. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t remember a week where I didn’t do it at least once. It’s embarrassing to say that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say it’s usually that. Sometimes I’m upset, yeah, but sometimes it’s just after work and I’m tired, or I’m in the parking lot thinking I should go home, and then I end up stopping somewhere anyway.\n\nStress is hard to tell, you know? Like everybody’s stressed. At the register people get mad over coupons or prices and then I’m carrying that around, but I don’t know if that’s the reason I eat like that. I haven’t really kept track of what I’m feeling right before it happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. After my shifts at the register, I’ll stop and buy these huge takeout orders—like, not just one meal, it’s burgers and fries and chicken, sometimes desserts too, like it’s for a whole family.\n\nAnd I’ll tell myself I’m just gonna have some of it before I head home, but then I’m sitting there in the car or wherever, and most of it’s gone. It’s embarrassing, because I’ll be thinking about how the person at the counter probably knows it’s all for me, or how I should’ve just gone straight home after work… but yeah, it’s a lot of food, and it happens pretty quick.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself from continuing to eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much it. Once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll be in my head saying, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hands are already reaching for the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can know I should stop, like really know it, and still keep going. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. Like, for the last several months, probably three or four nights in most weeks.\n\nI keep trying to tell myself it was just a bad week, but then another week goes by and it happens again. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t remember a week where I didn’t do it at least once. It’s embarrassing to say that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say it’s usually that. Sometimes I’m upset, yeah, but sometimes it’s just after work and I’m tired, or I’m in the parking lot thinking I should go home, and then I end up stopping somewhere anyway.\n\nStress is hard to tell, you know? Like everybody’s stressed. At the register people get mad over coupons or prices and then I’m carrying that around, but I don’t know if that’s the reason I eat like that. I haven’t really kept track of what I’m feeling right before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling upset or emotional when you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sometimes I probably am, but I don’t always notice it like that in the moment.\n\nLike after work, my feet hurt and I’m just kind of… worn down, but I don’t know if that counts as emotional. I’ve had customers yell at me over dumb stuff, like one lady last week got mad because her card declined and somehow that was my fault, and then I’m thinking about that on the drive home—but I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m upset, so I’m gonna eat.” It’s more like I just end up doing it.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to answer “usually.” I haven’t paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean… maybe? I don’t really know how to pin it down like that.

And then I’ll sit there and eat almost all of it myself before I even go home.\n\nIt’s weird because at work I’m scanning groceries all day, and I see people buying normal dinners, you know, and I’ll be thinking about what I’m gonna get after. Anyway… that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating these large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, but yeah.

It’s not even just feeling full or whatever, it’s like… looking at all the wrappers and bags and thinking, “Why did I do that?” So I’ll eat in the car sometimes, like in a parking lot, because I don’t want anybody seeing how much I got. And then I feel stupid for hiding it, like I’m some kid sneaking candy or something. But yeah, ashamed is probably the word.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess it’s the eating. Like after my cashier shifts, I’ll be tired and I’ll stop and buy these really huge takeout orders—burgers, fries, chicken, desserts, just way too much, like enough for a whole family. And then I’ll sit there and eat almost all of it myself before I even go home.\n\nIt’s weird because at work I’m scanning groceries all day, and I see people buying normal dinners, you know, and I’ll be thinking about what I’m gonna get after. Anyway… that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating these large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Like, afterward I mostly just feel kind of… blank, or annoyed with myself maybe, but then I’ll start thinking about stupid stuff like whether I left my work shirt in the dryer or if my manager changed the schedule again. So I’m not sure. What do you mean by guilty, like ashamed, or just regretting it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me, I guess. Like once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already reaching for the next thing.\n\nAnd it’s not like I’m even enjoying every bite, you know? Sometimes I’m thinking about the receipt, or if the fries got cold, or some random customer from work who was rude about coupons… but I’m still eating. So yeah, stopping doesn’t really feel like something I’m in charge of once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I’ll be sitting there telling myself to stop, like, “Demetrius, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already grabbing the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s like after the first bite something just shuts off in my head. And then I’m thinking about other random stuff too, like whether I still have my name tag in my pocket or if the fries are getting cold, but I’m still eating. So… yeah, I don’t feel like I’m really controlling how much once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I don’t usually, like, time myself or anything. Sometimes it feels like I look down and a bunch of it is just gone, but I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not paying attention.\n\nLike at work, I’m always having to eat on breaks kind of quick anyway, because if the line gets backed up they’ll call somebody over, and then I’m thinking about that kind of stuff even when I’m home. But during those takeout times… I guess I haven’t really noticed the speed so much. I notice more that I’m just kind of doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of why I’m here. After my shifts at the register, I’ll stop and buy these huge takeout orders—like, not just one meal, it’s burgers and fries and chicken, sometimes desserts too, like it’s for a whole family.\n\nAnd I’ll tell myself I’m just gonna have some of it before I head home, but then I’m sitting there in the car or wherever, and most of it’s gone. It’s embarrassing, because I’ll be thinking about how the person at the counter probably knows it’s all for me, or how I should’ve just gone straight home after work… but yeah, it’s a lot of food, and it happens pretty quick.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself from continuing to eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much it. Once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll be in my head saying, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hands are already reaching for the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can know I should stop, like really know it, and still keep going. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. Like, for the last several months, probably three or four nights in most weeks.\n\nI keep trying to tell myself it was just a bad week, but then another week goes by and it happens again. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t remember a week where I didn’t do it at least once. It’s embarrassing to say that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say it’s usually that. Sometimes I’m upset, yeah, but sometimes it’s just after work and I’m tired, or I’m in the parking lot thinking I should go home, and then I end up stopping somewhere anyway.\n\nStress is hard to tell, you know? Like everybody’s stressed. At the register people get mad over coupons or prices and then I’m carrying that around, but I don’t know if that’s the reason I eat like that. I haven’t really kept track of what I’m feeling right before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling upset or emotional when you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sometimes I probably am, but I don’t always notice it like that in the moment.\n\nLike after work, my feet hurt and I’m just kind of… worn down, but I don’t know if that counts as emotional. I don’t really know how to pin it down like that.\n\nSometimes something happens and I’m bothered, but then by the time I’m eating, I’m not thinking, “this is because I’m emotional.” I’m just thinking about getting food, or not even thinking much at all. Yeah… I mean, no, not really. It’s weird because at the start I’ll tell myself, like, “Okay, just this much,” or “I’m not gonna eat all of it,” and then once I take that first bite it’s like something in me just kind of clicks off.

I mean, I’ll be sitting there telling myself to stop, like, “Demetrius, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already grabbing the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s like after the first bite something just shuts off in my head. And then I’m thinking about other random stuff too, like whether I still have my name tag in my pocket or if the fries are getting cold, but I’m still eating. So… yeah, I don’t feel like I’m really controlling how much once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I don’t usually, like, time myself or anything. Sometimes it feels like I look down and a bunch of it is just gone, but I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not paying attention.\n\nLike at work, I’m always having to eat on breaks kind of quick anyway, because if the line gets backed up they’ll call somebody over, and then I’m thinking about that kind of stuff even when I’m home. But during those takeout times… I guess I haven’t really noticed the speed so much. I notice more that I’m just kind of doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of why I’m here. After my shifts at the register, I’ll stop and buy these huge takeout orders—like, not just one meal, it’s burgers and fries and chicken, sometimes desserts too, like it’s for a whole family.\n\nAnd I’ll tell myself I’m just gonna have some of it before I head home, but then I’m sitting there in the car or wherever, and most of it’s gone. It’s embarrassing, because I’ll be thinking about how the person at the counter probably knows it’s all for me, or how I should’ve just gone straight home after work… but yeah, it’s a lot of food, and it happens pretty quick.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself from continuing to eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much it. Once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll be in my head saying, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hands are already reaching for the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can know I should stop, like really know it, and still keep going. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. Like, for the last several months, probably three or four nights in most weeks.\n\nI keep trying to tell myself it was just a bad week, but then another week goes by and it happens again. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t remember a week where I didn’t do it at least once. It’s embarrassing to say that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say it’s usually that. Sometimes I’m upset, yeah, but sometimes it’s just after work and I’m tired, or I’m in the parking lot thinking I should go home, and then I end up stopping somewhere anyway.\n\nStress is hard to tell, you know? Like everybody’s stressed. At the register people get mad over coupons or prices and then I’m carrying that around, but I don’t know if that’s the reason I eat like that. I haven’t really kept track of what I’m feeling right before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling upset or emotional when you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sometimes I probably am, but I don’t always notice it like that in the moment.\n\nLike after work, my feet hurt and I’m just kind of… worn down, but I don’t know if that counts as emotional. I’ve had customers yell at me over dumb stuff, like one lady last week got mad because her card declined and somehow that was my fault, and then I’m thinking about that on the drive home—but I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m upset, so I’m gonna eat.” It’s more like I just end up doing it.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to answer “usually.” I haven’t paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating large amounts of food is ever triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t really know how to pin it down like that.\n\nSometimes something happens and I’m bothered, but then by the time I’m eating, I’m not thinking, “this is because I’m emotional.” I’m just thinking about getting food, or not even thinking much at all. And then afterward I start replaying stuff in my head, like something a customer said or some problem I need to deal with, but I don’t know if that caused it or if I’m just looking for a reason.\n\nSorry, I know that’s kind of all over the place. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear trigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed of yourself after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, but yeah.\n\nIt’s not even just feeling full or whatever, it’s like… looking at all the wrappers and bags and thinking, “Why did I do that?” So I’ll eat in the car sometimes, like in a parking lot, because I don’t want anybody seeing how much I got. Or if I bring it home, I’ll wait until my wife’s asleep. Then I’m trying to shove the bags and receipts way down in the trash so she doesn’t notice, which is ridiculous because it’s my own trash, you know?\n\nAnd then I feel stupid for hiding it, like I’m some kid sneaking candy or something. But yeah, ashamed is probably the word.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those episodes where you consume large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, no, not really. It’s weird because at the start I’ll tell myself, like, “Okay, just this much,” or “I’m not gonna eat all of it,” and then once I take that first bite it’s like something in me just kind of clicks off.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic, but I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop. You don’t need any more,” and my hands are still just reaching for the next thing. Like I’m arguing with myself but not actually stopping.\n\nIt reminds me of when the register freezes at work and you keep pressing the same button even though you know it’s not doing anything—sorry, that’s probably a dumb comparison. But yeah, in those moments I don’t feel like I’m really in control.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. Like, for the last several months, probably three or four nights in most weeks.\n\nI keep trying to tell myself it was just a bad week, but then another week goes by and it happens again. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t remember a week where I didn’t do it at least once. It’s embarrassing to say that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say it’s usually that. Sometimes I’m upset, yeah, but sometimes it’s just after work and I’m tired, or I’m in the parking lot thinking I should go home, and then I end up stopping somewhere anyway.\n\nStress is hard to tell, you know? Like everybody’s stressed. At the register people get mad over coupons or prices and then I’m carrying that around, but I don’t know if that’s the reason I eat like that. I haven’t really kept track of what I’m feeling right before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling upset or emotional when you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sometimes I probably am, but I don’t always notice it like that in the moment.\n\nLike after work, my feet hurt and I’m just kind of… worn down, but I don’t know if that counts as emotional. I’ve had customers yell at me over dumb stuff, like one lady last week got mad because her card declined and somehow that was my fault, and then I’m thinking about that on the drive home—but I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m upset, so I’m gonna eat.” It’s more like I just end up doing it.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to answer “usually.” I haven’t paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating large amounts of food is ever triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t really know how to pin it down like that.\n\nSometimes something happens and I’m bothered, but then by the time I’m eating, I’m not thinking, “this is because I’m emotional.” I’m just thinking about getting food, or not even thinking much at all. And then afterward I start replaying stuff in my head, like something a customer said or some problem I need to deal with, but I don’t know if that caused it or if I’m just looking for a reason.\n\nSorry, I know that’s kind of all over the place. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear trigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed of yourself after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, but yeah.\n\nIt’s not even just feeling full or whatever, it’s like… looking at all the wrappers and bags and thinking, “Why did I do that?” So I’ll eat in the car sometimes, like in a parking lot, because I don’t want anybody seeing how much I got. Or if I bring it home, I’ll wait until my wife’s asleep. Then I’m trying to shove the bags and receipts way down in the trash so she doesn’t notice, which is ridiculous because it’s my own trash, you know?\n\nAnd then I feel stupid for hiding it, like I’m some kid sneaking candy or something. But yeah, ashamed is probably the word.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those episodes where you consume large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, no, not really. It’s weird because at the start I’ll tell myself, like, “Okay, just this much,” or “I’m not gonna eat all of it,” and then once I take that first bite it’s like something in me just kind of clicks off.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic, but I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop. You don’t need any more,” and my hands are still just reaching for the next thing. Like I’m arguing with myself but not actually stopping.\n\nIt reminds me of when the register freezes at work and you keep pressing the same button even though you know it’s not doing anything—sorry, that’s probably a dumb comparison. But yeah, in those moments I don’t feel like I’m really in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over your eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much what it feels like. Like I was saying, I can go in thinking I’m gonna keep it normal, and then once I take that first bite, something just kind of shuts off in my head.\n\nI’ll even be telling myself, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” but my hands are still grabbing the next thing. It’s like when I’m at work and the scanner won’t read something, and you keep swiping it even though you know it’s not helping—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, during those times, it really doesn’t feel like I have control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really paid attention to the speed, honestly.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’m just kind of focused on the food being there, and then I’ll look up and it’s like, okay, that’s gone. But I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not thinking about it. Like at work, when the line gets backed up and you’re scanning stuff and suddenly it’s been an hour—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed it enough to say for sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m not really sure, honestly. Like, maybe? But I don’t remember sitting there thinking, “Wow, I’m eating faster than normal.”

It’s more like I sort of lose track, and then I notice a bunch of food is gone. But whether that’s because I was eating fast or because I wasn’t paying attention… I don’t know.

Like when I’m on autopilot at the register and suddenly I’ve bagged three people’s stuff without really remembering each item—sorry, I keep comparing it to work. But yeah, I haven’t really noticed the speed enough to say for sure.

And then I’ll sit there and eat almost all of it myself before I even go home.\n\nIt’s weird because at work I’m scanning groceries all day, and I see people buying normal dinners, you know, and I’ll be thinking about what I’m gonna get after. Anyway… that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating these large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. So… yeah, I don’t feel like I’m really controlling how much once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I don’t usually, like, time myself or anything. Sometimes it feels like I look down and a bunch of it is just gone, but I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not paying attention.\n\nLike at work, I’m always having to eat on breaks kind of quick anyway, because if the line gets backed up they’ll call somebody over, and then I’m thinking about that kind of stuff even when I’m home. But during those takeout times… I guess I haven’t really noticed the speed so much. I notice more that I’m just kind of doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of why I’m here. It’s embarrassing, because I’ll be thinking about how the person at the counter probably knows it’s all for me, or how I should’ve just gone straight home after work… but yeah, it’s a lot of food, and it happens pretty quick.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself from continuing to eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much it. Once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll be in my head saying, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hands are already reaching for the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can know I should stop, like really know it, and still keep going. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. Like, for the last several months, probably three or four nights in most weeks.\n\nI keep trying to tell myself it was just a bad week, but then another week goes by and it happens again. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t remember a week where I didn’t do it at least once. It’s embarrassing to say that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say it’s usually that. Sometimes I’m upset, yeah, but sometimes it’s just after work and I’m tired, or I’m in the parking lot thinking I should go home, and then I end up stopping somewhere anyway.\n\nStress is hard to tell, you know? Like everybody’s stressed. At the register people get mad over coupons or prices and then I’m carrying that around, but I don’t know if that’s the reason I eat like that. I haven’t really kept track of what I’m feeling right before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling upset or emotional when you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sometimes I probably am, but I don’t always notice it like that in the moment.\n\nLike after work, my feet hurt and I’m just kind of… worn down, but I don’t know if that counts as emotional. I’ve had customers yell at me over dumb stuff, like one lady last week got mad because her card declined and somehow that was my fault, and then I’m thinking about that on the drive home—but I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m upset, so I’m gonna eat.” It’s more like I just end up doing it.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to answer “usually.” I haven’t paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating large amounts of food is ever triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t really know how to pin it down like that.\n\nSometimes something happens and I’m bothered, but then by the time I’m eating, I’m not thinking, “this is because I’m emotional.” I’m just thinking about getting food, or not even thinking much at all. And then afterward I start replaying stuff in my head, like something a customer said or some problem I need to deal with, but I don’t know if that caused it or if I’m just looking for a reason.\n\nSorry, I know that’s kind of all over the place. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear trigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed of yourself after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess it’s the eating. Like after my cashier shifts, I’ll be tired and I’ll stop and buy these really huge takeout orders—burgers, fries, chicken, desserts, just way too much, like enough for a whole family. And then I’ll sit there and eat almost all of it myself before I even go home.\n\nIt’s weird because at work I’m scanning groceries all day, and I see people buying normal dinners, you know, and I’ll be thinking about what I’m gonna get after. Anyway… that’s kind of why I’m here.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating these large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I’d call it guilt exactly. Like, afterward I mostly just feel kind of… blank, or annoyed with myself maybe, but then I’ll start thinking about stupid stuff like whether I left my work shirt in the dryer or if my manager changed the schedule again. So I’m not sure. What do you mean by guilty, like ashamed, or just regretting it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you can stop eating once you've started on those large takeout orders?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me, I guess. Like once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already reaching for the next thing.\n\nAnd it’s not like I’m even enjoying every bite, you know? Sometimes I’m thinking about the receipt, or if the fries got cold, or some random customer from work who was rude about coupons… but I’m still eating. So yeah, stopping doesn’t really feel like something I’m in charge of once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I’ll be sitting there telling myself to stop, like, “Demetrius, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already grabbing the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s like after the first bite something just shuts off in my head. And then I’m thinking about other random stuff too, like whether I still have my name tag in my pocket or if the fries are getting cold, but I’m still eating. So… yeah, I don’t feel like I’m really controlling how much once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I don’t usually, like, time myself or anything. Sometimes it feels like I look down and a bunch of it is just gone, but I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not paying attention.\n\nLike at work, I’m always having to eat on breaks kind of quick anyway, because if the line gets backed up they’ll call somebody over, and then I’m thinking about that kind of stuff even when I’m home. But during those takeout times… I guess I haven’t really noticed the speed so much. I notice more that I’m just kind of doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of why I’m here. After my shifts at the register, I’ll stop and buy these huge takeout orders—like, not just one meal, it’s burgers and fries and chicken, sometimes desserts too, like it’s for a whole family.\n\nAnd I’ll tell myself I’m just gonna have some of it before I head home, but then I’m sitting there in the car or wherever, and most of it’s gone. It’s embarrassing, because I’ll be thinking about how the person at the counter probably knows it’s all for me, or how I should’ve just gone straight home after work… but yeah, it’s a lot of food, and it happens pretty quick.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself from continuing to eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much it. Once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll be in my head saying, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hands are already reaching for the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can know I should stop, like really know it, and still keep going. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. Like, for the last several months, probably three or four nights in most weeks.\n\nI keep trying to tell myself it was just a bad week, but then another week goes by and it happens again. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t remember a week where I didn’t do it at least once. It’s embarrassing to say that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say it’s usually that. Sometimes I’m upset, yeah, but sometimes it’s just after work and I’m tired, or I’m in the parking lot thinking I should go home, and then I end up stopping somewhere anyway.\n\nStress is hard to tell, you know? Like everybody’s stressed. At the register people get mad over coupons or prices and then I’m carrying that around, but I don’t know if that’s the reason I eat like that. I haven’t really kept track of what I’m feeling right before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling upset or emotional when you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sometimes I probably am, but I don’t always notice it like that in the moment.\n\nLike after work, my feet hurt and I’m just kind of… worn down, but I don’t know if that counts as emotional. I’ve had customers yell at me over dumb stuff, like one lady last week got mad because her card declined and somehow that was my fault, and then I’m thinking about that on the drive home—but I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m upset, so I’m gonna eat.” It’s more like I just end up doing it.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to answer “usually.” I haven’t paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating large amounts of food is ever triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t really know how to pin it down like that.\n\nSometimes something happens and I’m bothered, but then by the time I’m eating, I’m not thinking, “this is because I’m emotional.” I’m just thinking about getting food, or not even thinking much at all. And then afterward I start replaying stuff in my head, like something a customer said or some problem I need to deal with, but I don’t know if that caused it or if I’m just looking for a reason.\n\nSorry, I know that’s kind of all over the place. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear trigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed of yourself after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, but yeah.\n\nIt’s not even just feeling full or whatever, it’s like… looking at all the wrappers and bags and thinking, “Why did I do that?” So I’ll eat in the car sometimes, like in a parking lot, because I don’t want anybody seeing how much I got. Or if I bring it home, I’ll wait until my wife’s asleep. Then I’m trying to shove the bags and receipts way down in the trash so she doesn’t notice, which is ridiculous because it’s my own trash, you know?\n\nAnd then I feel stupid for hiding it, like I’m some kid sneaking candy or something. But yeah, ashamed is probably the word.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those episodes where you consume large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, no, not really. It’s weird because at the start I’ll tell myself, like, “Okay, just this much,” or “I’m not gonna eat all of it,” and then once I take that first bite it’s like something in me just kind of clicks off.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic, but I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop. You don’t need any more,” and my hands are still just reaching for the next thing. Like I’m arguing with myself but not actually stopping.\n\nIt reminds me of when the register freezes at work and you keep pressing the same button even though you know it’s not doing anything—sorry, that’s probably a dumb comparison. But yeah, in those moments I don’t feel like I’m really in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over your eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much what it feels like. Like I was saying, I can go in thinking I’m gonna keep it normal, and then once I take that first bite, something just kind of shuts off in my head.\n\nI’ll even be telling myself, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” but my hands are still grabbing the next thing. Like once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already reaching for the next thing.\n\nAnd it’s not like I’m even enjoying every bite, you know? Sometimes I’m thinking about the receipt, or if the fries got cold, or some random customer from work who was rude about coupons… but I’m still eating. So yeah, stopping doesn’t really feel like something I’m in charge of once I start.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, I’ll be sitting there telling myself to stop, like, “Demetrius, you don’t need any more,” and then my hand is already grabbing the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s like after the first bite something just shuts off in my head. And then I’m thinking about other random stuff too, like whether I still have my name tag in my pocket or if the fries are getting cold, but I’m still eating. So… yeah, I don’t feel like I’m really controlling how much once it starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. I don’t usually, like, time myself or anything. Sometimes it feels like I look down and a bunch of it is just gone, but I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not paying attention.\n\nLike at work, I’m always having to eat on breaks kind of quick anyway, because if the line gets backed up they’ll call somebody over, and then I’m thinking about that kind of stuff even when I’m home. But during those takeout times… I guess I haven’t really noticed the speed so much. I notice more that I’m just kind of doing it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s kind of why I’m here. After my shifts at the register, I’ll stop and buy these huge takeout orders—like, not just one meal, it’s burgers and fries and chicken, sometimes desserts too, like it’s for a whole family.\n\nAnd I’ll tell myself I’m just gonna have some of it before I head home, but then I’m sitting there in the car or wherever, and most of it’s gone. It’s embarrassing, because I’ll be thinking about how the person at the counter probably knows it’s all for me, or how I should’ve just gone straight home after work… but yeah, it’s a lot of food, and it happens pretty quick.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop yourself from continuing to eat during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much it. Once I take that first bite, it’s like something in me clicks off. I’ll be in my head saying, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” and then my hands are already reaching for the next thing anyway.\n\nIt’s weird because I can know I should stop, like really know it, and still keep going. Like when the register drawer jams at work and you know you’re supposed to call a manager but you keep trying to fix it yourself—sorry, that’s not exactly the same thing. But yeah… in the moment, I don’t feel like I can make myself stop.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen?\n\nPatient: Lately it’s been… a lot. Like, for the last several months, probably three or four nights in most weeks.\n\nI keep trying to tell myself it was just a bad week, but then another week goes by and it happens again. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t remember a week where I didn’t do it at least once. It’s embarrassing to say that out loud.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food usually happen when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I mean… I don’t know if I can say it’s usually that. Sometimes I’m upset, yeah, but sometimes it’s just after work and I’m tired, or I’m in the parking lot thinking I should go home, and then I end up stopping somewhere anyway.\n\nStress is hard to tell, you know? Like everybody’s stressed. At the register people get mad over coupons or prices and then I’m carrying that around, but I don’t know if that’s the reason I eat like that. I haven’t really kept track of what I’m feeling right before it happens.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually feeling upset or emotional when you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sometimes I probably am, but I don’t always notice it like that in the moment.\n\nLike after work, my feet hurt and I’m just kind of… worn down, but I don’t know if that counts as emotional. I’ve had customers yell at me over dumb stuff, like one lady last week got mad because her card declined and somehow that was my fault, and then I’m thinking about that on the drive home—but I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m upset, so I’m gonna eat.” It’s more like I just end up doing it.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to answer “usually.” I haven’t paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating large amounts of food is ever triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t really know how to pin it down like that.\n\nSometimes something happens and I’m bothered, but then by the time I’m eating, I’m not thinking, “this is because I’m emotional.” I’m just thinking about getting food, or not even thinking much at all. And then afterward I start replaying stuff in my head, like something a customer said or some problem I need to deal with, but I don’t know if that caused it or if I’m just looking for a reason.\n\nSorry, I know that’s kind of all over the place. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear trigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed of yourself after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, but yeah.\n\nIt’s not even just feeling full or whatever, it’s like… looking at all the wrappers and bags and thinking, “Why did I do that?” So I’ll eat in the car sometimes, like in a parking lot, because I don’t want anybody seeing how much I got. Or if I bring it home, I’ll wait until my wife’s asleep. Then I’m trying to shove the bags and receipts way down in the trash so she doesn’t notice, which is ridiculous because it’s my own trash, you know?\n\nAnd then I feel stupid for hiding it, like I’m some kid sneaking candy or something. But yeah, ashamed is probably the word.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those episodes where you consume large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, no, not really. It’s weird because at the start I’ll tell myself, like, “Okay, just this much,” or “I’m not gonna eat all of it,” and then once I take that first bite it’s like something in me just kind of clicks off.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic, but I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop. You don’t need any more,” and my hands are still just reaching for the next thing. Like I’m arguing with myself but not actually stopping.\n\nIt reminds me of when the register freezes at work and you keep pressing the same button even though you know it’s not doing anything—sorry, that’s probably a dumb comparison. But yeah, in those moments I don’t feel like I’m really in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over your eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much what it feels like. Like I was saying, I can go in thinking I’m gonna keep it normal, and then once I take that first bite, something just kind of shuts off in my head.\n\nI’ll even be telling myself, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” but my hands are still grabbing the next thing. It’s like when I’m at work and the scanner won’t read something, and you keep swiping it even though you know it’s not helping—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, during those times, it really doesn’t feel like I have control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really paid attention to the speed, honestly.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’m just kind of focused on the food being there, and then I’ll look up and it’s like, okay, that’s gone. But I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not thinking about it. Like at work, when the line gets backed up and you’re scanning stuff and suddenly it’s been an hour—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed it enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating more quickly than usual during those episodes where you consume large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. Like, maybe? But I don’t remember sitting there thinking, “Wow, I’m eating faster than normal.”\n\nIt’s more like I sort of lose track, and then I notice a bunch of food is gone. But whether that’s because I was eating fast or because I wasn’t paying attention… I don’t know.\n\nLike when I’m on autopilot at the register and suddenly I’ve bagged three people’s stuff without really remembering each item—sorry, I keep comparing it to work. But yeah, I haven’t really noticed the speed enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do these episodes of eating large amounts of food typically happen?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s… probably three or four nights. Like after work, once I’m home and it’s quiet, that’s when it usually happens.\n\nIt’s been like that for the last few months, I guess. I mean, sometimes I probably am, but I don’t always notice it like that in the moment.\n\nLike after work, my feet hurt and I’m just kind of… worn down, but I don’t know if that counts as emotional. I’ve had customers yell at me over dumb stuff, like one lady last week got mad because her card declined and somehow that was my fault, and then I’m thinking about that on the drive home—but I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m upset, so I’m gonna eat.” It’s more like I just end up doing it.\n\nSo… I don’t really know how to answer “usually.” I haven’t paid close enough attention, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating large amounts of food is ever triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe? I don’t really know how to pin it down like that.\n\nSometimes something happens and I’m bothered, but then by the time I’m eating, I’m not thinking, “this is because I’m emotional.” I’m just thinking about getting food, or not even thinking much at all. And then afterward I start replaying stuff in my head, like something a customer said or some problem I need to deal with, but I don’t know if that caused it or if I’m just looking for a reason.\n\nSorry, I know that’s kind of all over the place. I guess I haven’t really noticed a clear trigger.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed of yourself after eating those large takeout orders by yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, but yeah.\n\nIt’s not even just feeling full or whatever, it’s like… looking at all the wrappers and bags and thinking, “Why did I do that?” So I’ll eat in the car sometimes, like in a parking lot, because I don’t want anybody seeing how much I got. Or if I bring it home, I’ll wait until my wife’s asleep. Then I’m trying to shove the bags and receipts way down in the trash so she doesn’t notice, which is ridiculous because it’s my own trash, you know?\n\nAnd then I feel stupid for hiding it, like I’m some kid sneaking candy or something. But yeah, ashamed is probably the word.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during those episodes where you consume large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, no, not really. It’s weird because at the start I’ll tell myself, like, “Okay, just this much,” or “I’m not gonna eat all of it,” and then once I take that first bite it’s like something in me just kind of clicks off.\n\nAnd I know that sounds dramatic, but I’ll literally be thinking, “Demetrius, stop. You don’t need any more,” and my hands are still just reaching for the next thing. Like I’m arguing with myself but not actually stopping.\n\nIt reminds me of when the register freezes at work and you keep pressing the same button even though you know it’s not doing anything—sorry, that’s probably a dumb comparison. But yeah, in those moments I don’t feel like I’m really in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have no control over your eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yeah, that’s pretty much what it feels like. Like I was saying, I can go in thinking I’m gonna keep it normal, and then once I take that first bite, something just kind of shuts off in my head.\n\nI’ll even be telling myself, “Demetrius, stop, you don’t need any more,” but my hands are still grabbing the next thing. It’s like when I’m at work and the scanner won’t read something, and you keep swiping it even though you know it’s not helping—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, during those times, it really doesn’t feel like I have control.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I haven’t really paid attention to the speed, honestly.\n\nI mean, sometimes I’m just kind of focused on the food being there, and then I’ll look up and it’s like, okay, that’s gone. But I don’t know if that means I’m eating fast or just not thinking about it. Like at work, when the line gets backed up and you’re scanning stuff and suddenly it’s been an hour—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nI guess I’m not sure. I haven’t noticed it enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're eating more quickly than usual during those episodes where you consume large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, honestly. Like, maybe? But I don’t remember sitting there thinking, “Wow, I’m eating faster than normal.”\n\nIt’s more like I sort of lose track, and then I notice a bunch of food is gone. But whether that’s because I was eating fast or because I wasn’t paying attention… I don’t know.\n\nLike when I’m on autopilot at the register and suddenly I’ve bagged three people’s stuff without really remembering each item—sorry, I keep comparing it to work. But yeah, I haven’t really noticed the speed enough to say for sure.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do these episodes of eating large amounts of food typically happen?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s… probably three or four nights. Like after work, once I’m home and it’s quiet, that’s when it usually happens.\n\nIt’s been like that for the last few months, I guess. Since those foreclosure calls started coming in, I honestly can’t think of a week where it didn’t happen at least once. I keep trying to tell myself, “Okay, this week I’m not doing that,” and then—yeah. But usually it’s more like three or four nights, not just one.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your episodes of eating large amounts of food are usually triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Or, I don’t know if “emotional” is the right word, but it’s usually when my head is loud, if that makes sense.\n\nA lot of the time I’m not even actually hungry when it starts. Like I might’ve already eaten dinner with my wife, and then later I’m still in the kitchen opening cabinets, looking for something. It’s not like my stomach is asking for food. It’s more like I’m trying to shut my brain off.\n\nSometimes I’ll tell myself I’m just getting a snack, but then it turns into more. I guess it does feel tied to how I’m feeling, yeah.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month do you think you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food?\n\nPatient: Um… per month? I guess if it’s three or four nights most weeks, then… I don’t know, maybe like twelve to sixteen times? I’m bad at doing it like that.\n\nIt’s been that way for the last several months, though. Since those foreclosure calls started, I honestly can’t remember a week where it didn’t happen at least once. Like even on weeks where I’m telling myself I’m going to get it together, there’s still at least one night where I end up doing it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
